{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01YE14z5qXyE6REuruUQp7uf","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01VYkQ4NPEDEDNFRHjv1Xz9H","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/first-order-system-identification.md","content":"# First-Order System Identification\n\n## Overview\nSystem identification is the process of determining mathematical models of dynamic systems from measured input-output data. For first-order systems, this involves estimating the process gain (K) and time constant (tau).\n\n## First-Order System Dynamics\n\nA first-order thermal system follows:\n```\ndT/dt = (1/tau) * (K * u + T_ambient - T)\n```\n\nAt steady state (dT/dt = 0):\n```\nT_ss = K * u + T_ambient\n```\n\nWhere:\n- T = temperature (°C)\n- u = input (heater power %)\n- K = process gain (°C per % power)\n- tau = time constant (seconds)\n- T_ambient = ambient temperature (°C)\n\n## Step Response Method\n\nThe most common method for identifying first-order systems is the step response:\n\n1. Start at equilibrium (heater off, T = T_ambient)\n2. Apply a constant input (e.g., 50% heater power)\n3. Record temperature vs. time for at least 3-5 time constants\n4. Fit the response to the theoretical curve\n\n### Response Equation\n\nFor a step input from 0 to u_step:\n```\nT(t) = T_ambient + K * u_step * (1 - exp(-t/tau))\n```\n\n### Parameter Estimation\n\nUsing curve fitting (scipy.optimize.curve_fit):\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\n\ndef first_order_response(t, K, tau, T0):\n    \"\"\"First-order step response model.\"\"\"\n    u_step = 50.0  # heater power used in test\n    return T0 + K * u_step * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n# Prepare data\ntimes = np.array([d['time'] for d in data])\ntemps = np.array([d['temperature'] for d in data])\n\n# Initial parameter guesses\nT0_guess = temps[0]\nK_guess = 0.1\ntau_guess = 30.0\n\n# Fit the model\nparams, covariance = curve_fit(\n    first_order_response,\n    times,\n    temps,\n    p0=[K_guess, tau_guess, T0_guess],\n    bounds=([0.01, 1.0, temps[0]-2], [1.0, 100.0, temps[0]+2])\n)\n\nK_est, tau_est, T0_est = params\n```\n\n### Quality Metrics\n\nCalculate R² to assess fit quality:\n```python\ndef calculate_r_squared(y_true, y_pred):\n    ss_res = np.sum((y_true - y_pred) ** 2)\n    ss_tot = np.sum((y_true - np.mean(y_true)) ** 2)\n    return 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n\ny_pred = first_order_response(times, K_est, tau_est, T0_est)\nr_squared = calculate_r_squared(temps, y_pred)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Duration**: Collect data for at least 30-60 seconds or 3τ\n2. **Data points**: Aim for 20+ points for reliable fitting\n3. **Input magnitude**: Use 40-60% heater power (mid-range)\n4. **Initial conditions**: Start at ambient temperature\n5. **Noise handling**: Use curve fitting rather than simple calculations\n\n## Common Issues\n\n- **Insufficient data**: Need enough time to see system response\n- **Poor initial guess**: Can cause curve_fit to fail\n- **Noise**: Use bounds and regularization in curve fitting\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":126,"cache_read_input_tokens":22029,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":126,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b027f611-89d7-4d0d-b364-9bf88c4dfb87","uuid":"1676ec5d-f150-49b2-81c3-2b92dc01ccfd"}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01YE14z5qXyE6REuruUQp7uf","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_018wwuGVpNJT25Xg8udeu9mX","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/pid-controller-tuning.md","content":"# PID Controller Tuning\n\n## Overview\nA PID (Proportional-Integral-Derivative) controller calculates a control signal based on the error between setpoint and measured value.\n\n## PID Equation\n\n```\nu(t) = Kp * e(t) + Ki * ∫e(τ)dτ + Kd * de(t)/dt\n```\n\nWhere:\n- u(t) = control output (heater power %)\n- e(t) = error (setpoint - measurement)\n- Kp = proportional gain\n- Ki = integral gain\n- Kd = derivative gain\n\n## Discrete Implementation\n\nFor digital control with timestep dt:\n\n```python\nclass PIDController:\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, dt):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.dt = dt\n\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n    def compute(self, setpoint, measurement):\n        error = setpoint - measurement\n\n        # Proportional term\n        P = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term\n        self.integral += error * self.dt\n        I = self.Ki * self.integral\n\n        # Derivative term\n        derivative = (error - self.prev_error) / self.dt\n        D = self.Kd * derivative\n\n        # Update state\n        self.prev_error = error\n\n        # Compute output\n        output = P + I + D\n        return output\n\n    def reset(self):\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n```\n\n## Tuning Methods\n\n### 1. Lambda Tuning (IMC Method)\n\nFor first-order systems with known K and tau, lambda tuning provides a systematic approach:\n\n```python\ndef lambda_tuning(K, tau, lambda_factor):\n    \"\"\"\n    IMC-based PID tuning for first-order systems.\n\n    Args:\n        K: Process gain\n        tau: Time constant\n        lambda_factor: Desired closed-loop time constant (tuning parameter)\n                      Larger = slower, more stable\n                      Smaller = faster, more aggressive\n\n    Returns:\n        Kp, Ki, Kd gains\n    \"\"\"\n    Kp = tau / (K * lambda_factor)\n    Ki = 1 / lambda_factor\n    Kd = 0  # Often not needed for thermal systems\n\n    return Kp, Ki, Kd\n```\n\nRecommended lambda values:\n- lambda = tau: Fast response, some overshoot\n- lambda = 2*tau: Balanced\n- lambda = 3*tau: Conservative, minimal overshoot\n\n### 2. Ziegler-Nichols (Closed-Loop)\n\n1. Set Ki = 0, Kd = 0\n2. Increase Kp until sustained oscillation occurs\n3. Record Ku (ultimate gain) and Pu (oscillation period)\n4. Calculate:\n   - Kp = 0.6 * Ku\n   - Ki = 2 * Kp / Pu\n   - Kd = Kp * Pu / 8\n\n### 3. Manual Tuning Guidelines\n\nStart with:\n1. Set Kd = 0, Ki = 0\n2. Increase Kp until acceptable response (some oscillation OK)\n3. Add Ki to eliminate steady-state error\n4. Add Kd only if needed for faster settling (often not needed for thermal)\n\n## Anti-Windup\n\nPrevent integral windup when output saturates:\n\n```python\ndef compute_with_antiwindup(self, setpoint, measurement, min_output=0, max_output=100):\n    error = setpoint - measurement\n\n    P = self.Kp * error\n\n    # Integral with anti-windup\n    self.integral += error * self.dt\n    I = self.Ki * self.integral\n\n    derivative = (error - self.prev_error) / self.dt\n    D = self.Kd * derivative\n\n    self.prev_error = error\n\n    # Calculate unclamped output\n    output = P + I + D\n\n    # Clamp output\n    clamped_output = np.clip(output, min_output, max_output)\n\n    # Back-calculate integral to prevent windup\n    if output != clamped_output:\n        self.integral -= (output - clamped_output) / self.Ki\n\n    return clamped_output\n```\n\n## Performance Specifications\n\nCommon control metrics:\n- **Rise time**: Time to reach 90% of setpoint\n- **Overshoot**: Maximum deviation above setpoint (%)\n- **Settling time**: Time to stay within ±2% of setpoint\n- **Steady-state error**: Final error after settling\n\n## Tuning for Thermal Systems\n\nThermal systems typically:\n- Have large time constants (slow)\n- Don't require derivative action\n- Benefit from conservative tuning\n- Need anti-windup due to power limits (0-100%)\n\nRecommended approach:\n1. Use lambda tuning with lambda = 1.5-2.0 * tau\n2. Set Kd = 0 initially\n3. Add integral action cautiously\n4. Implement anti-windup\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":126,"cache_read_input_tokens":22029,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":126,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b027f611-89d7-4d0d-b364-9bf88c4dfb87","uuid":"bf7129c5-30e5-4af5-aaea-48af8f53e5b6"}

For temperature control, key metrics include rise time, overshoot, settling time, and steady-state error.\n\n## Key Metrics\n\n### 1. Rise Time\nTime for the response to go from 10% to 90% of the final value (or from initial to 90% of setpoint).\n\n```python\ndef calculate_rise_time(times, temperatures, setpoint, initial_temp):\n    \"\"\"Calculate time to reach 90% of setpoint from initial temperature.\"\"\"\n    target_10 = initial_temp + 0.1 * (setpoint - initial_temp)\n    target_90 = initial_temp + 0.9 * (setpoint - initial_temp)\n\n    # Find when crossed 10% and 90%\n    idx_10 = np.where(np.array(temperatures) >= target_10)[0]\n    idx_90 = np.where(np.array(temperatures) >= target_90)[0]\n\n    if len(idx_10) == 0 or len(idx_90) == 0:\n        return None\n\n    t_10 = times[idx_10[0]]\n    t_90 = times[idx_90[0]]\n\n    return t_90 - t_10\n```\n\n### 2. Overshoot\nMaximum percentage the response exceeds the setpoint.\n\n```python\ndef calculate_overshoot(temperatures, setpoint):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage overshoot beyond setpoint.\"\"\"\n    max_temp = np.max(temperatures)\n\n    if max_temp <= setpoint:\n        return 0.0\n\n    overshoot = (max_temp - setpoint) / setpoint\n    return overshoot\n```\n\n### 3. Settling Time\nTime for the response to enter and stay within ±2% (or specified tolerance) of setpoint.\n\n```python\ndef calculate_settling_time(times, temperatures, setpoint, tolerance=0.02):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate settling time (time to stay within tolerance of setpoint).\n\n    Args:\n        times: Array of timestamps\n        temperatures: Array of temperature measurements\n        setpoint: Target temperature\n        tolerance: Settling tolerance (default ±2%)\n\n    Returns:\n        Settling time in seconds, or None if never settled\n    \"\"\"\n    temps = np.array(temperatures)\n    times = np.array(times)\n\n    tolerance_band = setpoint * tolerance\n    lower_bound = setpoint - tolerance_band\n    upper_bound = setpoint + tolerance_band\n\n    # Find when response first enters the tolerance band\n    in_band = (temps >= lower_bound) & (temps <= upper_bound)\n\n    if not np.any(in_band):\n        return None\n\n    # Find the last time it left the band\n    first_entry = np.where(in_band)[0][0]\n\n    # Check if it stays within band from this point\n    for i in range(first_entry, len(temps)):\n        if not in_band[i]:\n            first_entry = i + 1\n\n    # Alternative: find last violation\n    violations = np.where(~in_band)[0]\n    if len(violations) == 0:\n        settling_idx = 0\n    else:\n        settling_idx = violations[-1] + 1\n\n    if settling_idx >= len(times):\n        return None\n\n    return times[settling_idx]\n```\n\n### 4. Steady-State Error\nFinal error between setpoint and measured value after settling.\n\n```python\ndef calculate_steady_state_error(temperatures, setpoint, last_n=20):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate steady-state error from the last N measurements.\n\n    Args:\n        temperatures: Array of temperature measurements\n        setpoint: Target temperature\n        last_n: Number of final points to average\n\n    Returns:\n        Absolute steady-state error\n    \"\"\"\n    if len(temperatures) < last_n:\n        last_n = len(temperatures)\n\n    final_temps = temperatures[-last_n:]\n    avg_final = np.mean(final_temps)\n\n    return abs(setpoint - avg_final)\n```\n\n### 5. Maximum Temperature\nMaximum temperature reached during control.\n\n```python\ndef calculate_max_temp(temperatures):\n    \"\"\"Calculate maximum temperature reached.\"\"\"\n    return np.max(temperatures)\n```\n\n## Complete Metrics Calculation\n\n```python\ndef calculate_all_metrics(data, setpoint):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate all control performance metrics.\n\n    Args:\n        data: List of dicts with 'time' and 'temperature' keys\n        setpoint: Target temperature\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with all metrics\n    \"\"\"\n    times = np.array([d['time'] for d in data])\n    temps = np.array([d['temperature'] for d in data])\n\n    initial_temp = temps[0]\n\n    metrics = {\n        'rise_time': calculate_rise_time(times, temps, setpoint, initial_temp),\n        'overshoot': calculate_overshoot(temps, setpoint),\n        'settling_time': calculate_settling_time(times, temps, setpoint),\n        'steady_state_error': calculate_steady_state_error(temps, setpoint),\n        'max_temp': calculate_max_temp(temps)\n    }\n\n    return metrics\n```\n\n## Typical Requirements\n\nFor temperature control systems:\n- **Overshoot**: < 5-10% (avoid temperature spikes)\n- **Settling time**: < 2-3 minutes for HVAC\n- **Steady-state error**: < 0.5°C\n- **Max temperature**: Must not exceed safety limits\n\n## Improved Settling Time Algorithm\n\n```python\ndef calculate_settling_time_improved(times, temperatures, setpoint, tolerance=0.02, window=10):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate settling time with sliding window check.\n    Ensures system stays settled for a minimum duration.\n    \"\"\"\n    temps = np.array(temperatures)\n    times = np.array(times)\n\n    tolerance_value = abs(setpoint * tolerance)\n    lower = setpoint - tolerance_value\n    upper = setpoint + tolerance_value\n\n    in_band = (temps >= lower) & (temps <= upper)\n\n    # Find last index where system was outside band\n    for i in range(len(temps) - 1, -1, -1):\n        # Check if next 'window' points are all in band\n        if i + window < len(temps):\n            if np.all(in_band[i:i+window]):\n                return times[i]\n        else:\n            if np.all(in_band[i:]):\n                return times[i]\n\n    return None\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":126,"cache_read_input_tokens":22029,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":126,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b027f611-89d7-4d0d-b364-9bf88c4dfb87","uuid":"deec9065-ff74-4c8e-9699-3098559c185d"}

For first-order systems, this involves estimating the process gain (K) and time constant (tau).\n\n## First-Order System Dynamics\n\nA first-order thermal system follows:\n```\ndT/dt = (1/tau) * (K * u + T_ambient - T)\n```\n\nAt steady state (dT/dt = 0):\n```\nT_ss = K * u + T_ambient\n```\n\nWhere:\n- T = temperature (°C)\n- u = input (heater power %)\n- K = process gain (°C per % power)\n- tau = time constant (seconds)\n- T_ambient = ambient temperature (°C)\n\n## Step Response Method\n\nThe most common method for identifying first-order systems is the step response:\n\n1. Start at equilibrium (heater off, T = T_ambient)\n2. Apply a constant input (e.g., 50% heater power)\n3. Record temperature vs. time for at least 3-5 time constants\n4. Fit the response to the theoretical curve\n\n### Response Equation\n\nFor a step input from 0 to u_step:\n```\nT(t) = T_ambient + K * u_step * (1 - exp(-t/tau))\n```\n\n### Parameter Estimation\n\nUsing curve fitting (scipy.optimize.curve_fit):\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\n\ndef first_order_response(t, K, tau, T0):\n    \"\"\"First-order step response model.\"\"\"\n    u_step = 50.0  # heater power used in test\n    return T0 + K * u_step * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n# Prepare data\ntimes = np.array([d['time'] for d in data])\ntemps = np.array([d['temperature'] for d in data])\n\n# Initial parameter guesses\nT0_guess = temps[0]\nK_guess = 0.1\ntau_guess = 30.0\n\n# Fit the model\nparams, covariance = curve_fit(\n    first_order_response,\n    times,\n    temps,\n    p0=[K_guess, tau_guess, T0_guess],\n    bounds=([0.01, 1.0, temps[0]-2], [1.0, 100.0, temps[0]+2])\n)\n\nK_est, tau_est, T0_est = params\n```\n\n### Quality Metrics\n\nCalculate R² to assess fit quality:\n```python\ndef calculate_r_squared(y_true, y_pred):\n    ss_res = np.sum((y_true - y_pred) ** 2)\n    ss_tot = np.sum((y_true - np.mean(y_true)) ** 2)\n    return 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n\ny_pred = first_order_response(times, K_est, tau_est, T0_est)\nr_squared = calculate_r_squared(temps, y_pred)\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Duration**: Collect data for at least 30-60 seconds or 3τ\n2. **Data points**: Aim for 20+ points for reliable fitting\n3. **Input magnitude**: Use 40-60% heater power (mid-range)\n4. **Initial conditions**: Start at ambient temperature\n5. **Noise handling**: Use curve fitting rather than simple calculations\n\n## Common Issues\n\n- **Insufficient data**: Need enough time to see system response\n- **Poor initial guess**: Can cause curve_fit to fail\n- **Noise**: Use bounds and regularization in curve fitting\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_018wwuGVpNJT25Xg8udeu9mX","type":"tool_result","content":"File created successfully at: /root/environment/skills/pid-controller-tuning.md"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"b027f611-89d7-4d0d-b364-9bf88c4dfb87","uuid":"3cb6a81c-af5f-43d1-b64e-d7f8fe2fb984","tool_use_result":{"type":"create","filePath":"/root/environment/skills/pid-controller-tuning.md","content":"# PID Controller Tuning\n\n## Overview\nA PID (Proportional-Integral-Derivative) controller calculates a control signal based on the error between setpoint and measured value.\n\n## PID Equation\n\n```\nu(t) = Kp * e(t) + Ki * ∫e(τ)dτ + Kd * de(t)/dt\n```\n\nWhere:\n- u(t) = control output (heater power %)\n- e(t) = error (setpoint - measurement)\n- Kp = proportional gain\n- Ki = integral gain\n- Kd = derivative gain\n\n## Discrete Implementation\n\nFor digital control with timestep dt:\n\n```python\nclass PIDController:\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, dt):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.dt = dt\n\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n    def compute(self, setpoint, measurement):\n        error = setpoint - measurement\n\n        # Proportional term\n        P = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term\n        self.integral += error * self.dt\n        I = self.Ki * self.integral\n\n        # Derivative term\n        derivative = (error - self.prev_error) / self.dt\n        D = self.Kd * derivative\n\n        # Update state\n        self.prev_error = error\n\n        # Compute output\n        output = P + I + D\n        return output\n\n    def reset(self):\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n```\n\n## Tuning Methods\n\n### 1. Lambda Tuning (IMC Method)\n\nFor first-order systems with known K and tau, lambda tuning provides a systematic approach:\n\n```python\ndef lambda_tuning(K, tau, lambda_factor):\n    \"\"\"\n    IMC-based PID tuning for first-order systems.\n\n    Args:\n        K: Process gain\n        tau: Time constant\n        lambda_factor: Desired closed-loop time constant (tuning parameter)\n                      Larger = slower, more stable\n                      Smaller = faster, more aggressive\n\n    Returns:\n        Kp, Ki, Kd gains\n    \"\"\"\n    Kp = tau / (K * lambda_factor)\n    Ki = 1 / lambda_factor\n    Kd = 0  # Often not needed for thermal systems\n\n    return Kp, Ki, Kd\n```\n\nRecommended lambda values:\n- lambda = tau: Fast response, some overshoot\n- lambda = 2*tau: Balanced\n- lambda = 3*tau: Conservative, minimal overshoot\n\n### 2. Ziegler-Nichols (Closed-Loop)\n\n1. Set Ki = 0, Kd = 0\n2. Increase Kp until sustained oscillation occurs\n3. Record Ku (ultimate gain) and Pu (oscillation period)\n4. Calculate:\n   - Kp = 0.6 * Ku\n   - Ki = 2 * Kp / Pu\n   - Kd = Kp * Pu / 8\n\n### 3. Manual Tuning Guidelines\n\nStart with:\n1. Set Kd = 0, Ki = 0\n2. Increase Kp until acceptable response (some oscillation OK)\n3. Add Ki to eliminate steady-state error\n4. Add Kd only if needed for faster settling (often not needed for thermal)\n\n## Anti-Windup\n\nPrevent integral windup when output saturates:\n\n```python\ndef compute_with_antiwindup(self, setpoint, measurement, min_output=0, max_output=100):\n    error = setpoint - measurement\n\n    P = self.Kp * error\n\n    # Integral with anti-windup\n    self.integral += error * self.dt\n    I = self.Ki * self.integral\n\n    derivative = (error - self.prev_error) / self.dt\n    D = self.Kd * derivative\n\n    self.prev_error = error\n\n    # Calculate unclamped output\n    output = P + I + D\n\n    # Clamp output\n    clamped_output = np.clip(output, min_output, max_output)\n\n    # Back-calculate integral to prevent windup\n    if output != clamped_output:\n        self.integral -= (output - clamped_output) / self.Ki\n\n    return clamped_output\n```\n\n## Performance Specifications\n\nCommon control metrics:\n- **Rise time**: Time to reach 90% of setpoint\n- **Overshoot**: Maximum deviation above setpoint (%)\n- **Settling time**: Time to stay within ±2% of setpoint\n- **Steady-state error**: Final error after settling\n\n## Tuning for Thermal Systems\n\nThermal systems typically:\n- Have large time constants (slow)\n- Don't require derivative action\n- Benefit from conservative tuning\n- Need anti-windup due to power limits (0-100%)\n\nRecommended approach:\n1. Use lambda tuning with lambda = 1.5-2.0 * tau\n2. Set Kd = 0 initially\n3. Add integral action cautiously\n4. Implement anti-windup\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

For temperature control, key metrics include rise time, overshoot, settling time, and steady-state error.\n\n## Key Metrics\n\n### 1. Rise Time\nTime for the response to go from 10% to 90% of the final value (or from initial to 90% of setpoint).\n\n```python\ndef calculate_rise_time(times, temperatures, setpoint, initial_temp):\n    \"\"\"Calculate time to reach 90% of setpoint from initial temperature.\"\"\"\n    target_10 = initial_temp + 0.1 * (setpoint - initial_temp)\n    target_90 = initial_temp + 0.9 * (setpoint - initial_temp)\n\n    # Find when crossed 10% and 90%\n    idx_10 = np.where(np.array(temperatures) >= target_10)[0]\n    idx_90 = np.where(np.array(temperatures) >= target_90)[0]\n\n    if len(idx_10) == 0 or len(idx_90) == 0:\n        return None\n\n    t_10 = times[idx_10[0]]\n    t_90 = times[idx_90[0]]\n\n    return t_90 - t_10\n```\n\n### 2. Overshoot\nMaximum percentage the response exceeds the setpoint.\n\n```python\ndef calculate_overshoot(temperatures, setpoint):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage overshoot beyond setpoint.\"\"\"\n    max_temp = np.max(temperatures)\n\n    if max_temp <= setpoint:\n        return 0.0\n\n    overshoot = (max_temp - setpoint) / setpoint\n    return overshoot\n```\n\n### 3. Settling Time\nTime for the response to enter and stay within ±2% (or specified tolerance) of setpoint.\n\n```python\ndef calculate_settling_time(times, temperatures, setpoint, tolerance=0.02):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate settling time (time to stay within tolerance of setpoint).\n\n    Args:\n        times: Array of timestamps\n        temperatures: Array of temperature measurements\n        setpoint: Target temperature\n        tolerance: Settling tolerance (default ±2%)\n\n    Returns:\n        Settling time in seconds, or None if never settled\n    \"\"\"\n    temps = np.array(temperatures)\n    times = np.array(times)\n\n    tolerance_band = setpoint * tolerance\n    lower_bound = setpoint - tolerance_band\n    upper_bound = setpoint + tolerance_band\n\n    # Find when response first enters the tolerance band\n    in_band = (temps >= lower_bound) & (temps <= upper_bound)\n\n    if not np.any(in_band):\n        return None\n\n    # Find the last time it left the band\n    first_entry = np.where(in_band)[0][0]\n\n    # Check if it stays within band from this point\n    for i in range(first_entry, len(temps)):\n        if not in_band[i]:\n            first_entry = i + 1\n\n    # Alternative: find last violation\n    violations = np.where(~in_band)[0]\n    if len(violations) == 0:\n        settling_idx = 0\n    else:\n        settling_idx = violations[-1] + 1\n\n    if settling_idx >= len(times):\n        return None\n\n    return times[settling_idx]\n```\n\n### 4. Steady-State Error\nFinal error between setpoint and measured value after settling.\n\n```python\ndef calculate_steady_state_error(temperatures, setpoint, last_n=20):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate steady-state error from the last N measurements.\n\n    Args:\n        temperatures: Array of temperature measurements\n        setpoint: Target temperature\n        last_n: Number of final points to average\n\n    Returns:\n        Absolute steady-state error\n    \"\"\"\n    if len(temperatures) < last_n:\n        last_n = len(temperatures)\n\n    final_temps = temperatures[-last_n:]\n    avg_final = np.mean(final_temps)\n\n    return abs(setpoint - avg_final)\n```\n\n### 5. Maximum Temperature\nMaximum temperature reached during control.\n\n```python\ndef calculate_max_temp(temperatures):\n    \"\"\"Calculate maximum temperature reached.\"\"\"\n    return np.max(temperatures)\n```\n\n## Complete Metrics Calculation\n\n```python\ndef calculate_all_metrics(data, setpoint):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate all control performance metrics.\n\n    Args:\n        data: List of dicts with 'time' and 'temperature' keys\n        setpoint: Target temperature\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with all metrics\n    \"\"\"\n    times = np.array([d['time'] for d in data])\n    temps = np.array([d['temperature'] for d in data])\n\n    initial_temp = temps[0]\n\n    metrics = {\n        'rise_time': calculate_rise_time(times, temps, setpoint, initial_temp),\n        'overshoot': calculate_overshoot(temps, setpoint),\n        'settling_time': calculate_settling_time(times, temps, setpoint),\n        'steady_state_error': calculate_steady_state_error(temps, setpoint),\n        'max_temp': calculate_max_temp(temps)\n    }\n\n    return metrics\n```\n\n## Typical Requirements\n\nFor temperature control systems:\n- **Overshoot**: < 5-10% (avoid temperature spikes)\n- **Settling time**: < 2-3 minutes for HVAC\n- **Steady-state error**: < 0.5°C\n- **Max temperature**: Must not exceed safety limits\n\n## Improved Settling Time Algorithm\n\n```python\ndef calculate_settling_time_improved(times, temperatures, setpoint, tolerance=0.02, window=10):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate settling time with sliding window check.\n    Ensures system stays settled for a minimum duration.\n    \"\"\"\n    temps = np.array(temperatures)\n    times = np.array(times)\n\n    tolerance_value = abs(setpoint * tolerance)\n    lower = setpoint - tolerance_value\n    upper = setpoint + tolerance_value\n\n    in_band = (temps >= lower) & (temps <= upper)\n\n    # Find last index where system was outside band\n    for i in range(len(temps) - 1, -1, -1):\n        # Check if next 'window' points are all in band\n        if i + window < len(temps):\n            if np.all(in_band[i:i+window]):\n                return times[i]\n        else:\n            if np.all(in_band[i:]):\n                return times[i]\n\n    return None\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Initial Guess\nProvide good initial guesses to help convergence:\n```python\nparams, cov = curve_fit(model, x, y, p0=[guess_a, guess_b, guess_c])\n```\n\n### bounds - Parameter Constraints\nConstrain parameters to physically meaningful ranges:\n```python\n# bounds = ([lower_bounds], [upper_bounds])\nparams, cov = curve_fit(\n    model, x, y,\n    p0=[1.0, 1.0, 1.0],\n    bounds=([0, 0, 0], [10, 10, 10])  # all params between 0 and 10\n)\n```\n\n### maxfev - Maximum Function Evaluations\nIncrease if optimization doesn't converge:\n```python\nparams, cov = curve_fit(model, x, y, p0=[1, 1, 1], maxfev=5000)\n```\n\n### sigma - Data Uncertainties\nProvide weights based on measurement uncertainty:\n```python\n# Higher sigma = less weight\nuncertainties = np.array([0.1, 0.2, 0.1, 0.3, 0.1, 0.2])\nparams, cov = curve_fit(model, x, y, sigma=uncertainties)\n```\n\n## Return Values\n\n```python\nparams, covariance = curve_fit(model, x, y)\n\n# params: optimal parameter values\n# covariance: covariance matrix of parameters\n#   - diagonal elements are variances (uncertainties²)\n#   - off-diagonal show correlations\n\n# Extract standard errors\nparam_errors = np.sqrt(np.diag(covariance))\n```\n\n## First-Order System Example\n\n```python\ndef first_order_step_response(t, K, tau, T_initial):\n    \"\"\"\n    First-order system step response.\n\n    Args:\n        t: time array\n        K: process gain\n        tau: time constant\n        T_initial: initial temperature\n\n    Returns:\n        Temperature at time t\n    \"\"\"\n    u_step = 50.0  # step input magnitude\n    return T_initial + K * u_step * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n# Data from experiment\ntime_data = np.array([0, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5, 3.0])\ntemp_data = np.array([18.0, 18.2, 18.5, 18.9, 19.4, 19.9, 20.3])\n\n# Initial guesses\nK_guess = 0.1\ntau_guess = 30.0\nT0_guess = temp_data[0]\n\n# Fit with constraints\nparams, cov = curve_fit(\n    first_order_step_response,\n    time_data,\n    temp_data,\n    p0=[K_guess, tau_guess, T0_guess],\n    bounds=([0.01, 5.0, temp_data[0]-1], [0.5, 100.0, temp_data[0]+1])\n)\n\nK_est, tau_est, T0_est = params\n\n# Calculate confidence\nparam_std = np.sqrt(np.diag(cov))\nprint(f\"K = {K_est:.4f} ± {param_std[0]:.4f}\")\nprint(f\"tau = {tau_est:.2f} ± {param_std[1]:.2f}\")\n```\n\n## Error Handling\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.optimize import OptimizeWarning\nimport warnings\n\ntry:\n    params, cov = curve_fit(model, x, y, p0=[1, 1])\nexcept RuntimeError as e:\n    print(f\"Curve fitting failed: {e}\")\n    # Try with different initial guess or bounds\nexcept OptimizeWarning as w:\n    warnings.warn(f\"Optimization warning: {w}\")\n```\n\n## Evaluating Fit Quality\n\n```python\ndef evaluate_fit(x_data, y_data, y_fitted):\n    \"\"\"Calculate R² and RMSE.\"\"\"\n    # R-squared\n    ss_res = np.sum((y_data - y_fitted) ** 2)\n    ss_tot = np.sum((y_data - np.mean(y_data)) ** 2)\n    r_squared = 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n\n    # Root mean square error\n    rmse = np.sqrt(np.mean((y_data - y_fitted) ** 2))\n\n    return r_squared, rmse\n\n# After fitting\ny_fitted = model_function(x_data, *params)\nr2, rmse = evaluate_fit(x_data, y_data, y_fitted)\nprint(f\"R² = {r2:.4f}, RMSE = {rmse:.4f}\")\n```\n\n## Tips for Success\n\n1. **Good initial guesses**: Critical for convergence\n2. **Use bounds**: Prevent non-physical solutions\n3. **Check residuals**: Plot y_data vs y_fitted\n4. **Scale parameters**: If parameters differ by orders of magnitude, rescale\n5. **Sufficient data**: Need more data points than parameters\n6. **Time offset**: For step responses, ensure time starts at step application\n\n## Common Issues\n\n**Problem**: \"Optimal parameters not found\"\n- Solution: Improve initial guess, add bounds, increase maxfev\n\n**Problem**: Unrealistic parameter values\n- Solution: Add appropriate bounds, check model function\n\n**Problem**: Poor fit despite convergence\n- Solution: Model may not match data, check for systematic errors\n\n## Advanced: Weighted Fitting\n\nFor data with non-uniform noise:\n```python\n# Inverse variance weighting\nweights = 1.0 / measurement_std**2\nparams, cov = curve_fit(model, x, y, sigma=measurement_std, absolute_sigma=True)\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01Q1RRkCksAVJMx5bnGFgtzA","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01AwgYT1bHZbiHDZZK11mWD1","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/temperature_controller.py","content":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"\nTemperature Controller Implementation\n\nThis script performs:\n1. Calibration test to characterize room dynamics\n2. Parameter estimation from calibration data\n3. PID controller gain tuning\n4. Closed-loop control to reach setpoint\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\nfrom hvac_simulator import HVACSimulator\n\n\nclass PIDController:\n    \"\"\"PID controller with anti-windup.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, dt, min_output=0.0, max_output=100.0):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.dt = dt\n        self.min_output = min_output\n        self.max_output = max_output\n\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n    def compute(self, setpoint, measurement):\n        \"\"\"Compute PID output with anti-windup.\"\"\"\n        error = setpoint - measurement\n\n        # Proportional term\n        P = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term (will apply anti-windup below)\n        self.integral += error * self.dt\n        I = self.Ki * self.integral\n\n        # Derivative term\n        derivative = (error - self.prev_error) / self.dt\n        D = self.Kd * derivative\n\n        self.prev_error = error\n\n        # Compute unclamped output\n        output = P + I + D\n\n        # Apply output limits\n        clamped_output = np.clip(output, self.min_output, self.max_output)\n\n        # Anti-windup: back-calculate integral\n        if self.Ki != 0 and output != clamped_output:\n            self.integral -= (output - clamped_output) / self.Ki\n\n        return clamped_output\n\n    def reset(self):\n        \"\"\"Reset controller state.\"\"\"\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n\ndef run_calibration(sim, heater_power=50.0, duration=30.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run open-loop calibration test.\n\n    Args:\n        sim: HVACSimulator instance\n        heater_power: Constant heater power for test (%)\n        duration: Test duration (seconds)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with calibration log\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Running calibration test: {heater_power}% power for {duration}s\")\n\n    # Reset simulator\n    initial_temp = sim.reset()\n    print(f\"Initial temperature: {initial_temp:.2f}C\")\n\n    # Collect data with heater off first (initial condition)\n    data = []\n    data.append({\n        \"time\": 0.0,\n        \"temperature\": initial_temp,\n        \"heater_power\": 0.0\n    })\n\n    # Run open-loop test\n    results = sim.run_open_loop(heater_power, duration)\n\n    # Add results to data\n    for result in results:\n        data.append({\n            \"time\": result[\"time\"],\n            \"temperature\": result[\"temperature\"],\n            \"heater_power\": result[\"heater_power\"]\n        })\n\n    print(f\"Calibration complete: {len(data)} data points collected\")\n\n    calibration_log = {\n        \"phase\": \"calibration\",\n        \"heater_power_test\": heater_power,\n        \"data\": data\n    }\n\n    return calibration_log\n\n\ndef estimate_parameters(calibration_log):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate system parameters from calibration data.\n\n    Args:\n        calibration_log: Calibration log dictionary\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with estimated parameters\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"Estimating system parameters from calibration data...\")\n\n    data = calibration_log[\"data\"]\n    heater_power = calibration_log[\"heater_power_test\"]\n\n    # Extract time and temperature arrays (skip first point where heater is off)\n    times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data[1:]])\n    temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data[1:]])\n\n    # Adjust time to start from 0\n    times = times - times[0]\n\n    # Initial temperature (before step)\n    T0 = data[0][\"temperature\"]\n\n    # Define first-order step response model\n    def first_order_response(t, K, tau):\n        \"\"\"First-order step response: T(t) = T0 + K*u*(1 - exp(-t/tau))\"\"\"\n        return T0 + K * heater_power * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n    # Initial parameter guesses\n    K_guess = 0.1  # Process gain\n    tau_guess = 30.0  # Time constant\n\n    try:\n        # Fit the model\n        params, covariance = curve_fit(\n            first_order_response,\n            times,\n            temps,\n            p0=[K_guess, tau_guess],\n            bounds=([0.01, 5.0], [0.5, 100.0]),\n            maxfev=5000\n        )\n\n        K_est, tau_est = params\n\n        # Calculate fit quality\n        y_pred = first_order_response(times, K_est, tau_est)\n        ss_res = np.sum((temps - y_pred) ** 2)\n        ss_tot = np.sum((temps - np.mean(temps)) ** 2)\n        r_squared = 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n\n        # Fitting error (RMSE)\n        fitting_error = np.sqrt(np.mean((temps - y_pred) ** 2))\n\n        print(f\"Estimated K = {K_est:.4f}, tau = {tau_est:.2f}s\")\n        print(f\"R² = {r_squared:.4f}, RMSE = {fitting_error:.4f}\")\n\n        estimated_params = {\n            \"K\": round(K_est, 6),\n            \"tau\": round(tau_est, 4),\n            \"r_squared\": round(r_squared, 6),\n            \"fitting_error\": round(fitting_error, 6)\n        }\n\n        return estimated_params\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Parameter estimation failed: {e}\")\n        raise\n\n\ndef calculate_controller_gains(estimated_params, lambda_factor=1.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate PID controller gains using lambda tuning.\n\n    Args:\n        estimated_params: Dictionary with K and tau\n        lambda_factor: Tuning parameter (multiplier of tau)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with controller gains\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"Calculating controller gains...\")\n\n    K = estimated_params[\"K\"]\n    tau = estimated_params[\"tau\"]\n\n    # Lambda tuning (IMC-based)\n    lambda_val = lambda_factor * tau\n\n    Kp = tau / (K * lambda_val)\n    Ki = 1.0 / lambda_val\n    Kd = 0.0  # Not needed for thermal systems\n\n    print(f\"Controller gains: Kp={Kp:.4f}, Ki={Ki:.4f}, Kd={Kd:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Lambda = {lambda_val:.2f}s\")\n\n    tuned_gains = {\n        \"Kp\": round(Kp, 6),\n        \"Ki\": round(Ki, 6),\n        \"Kd\": round(Kd, 6),\n        \"lambda\": round(lambda_val, 4)\n    }\n\n    return tuned_gains\n\n\ndef run_closed_loop_control(sim, tuned_gains, setpoint, duration=150.0, start_time=0.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run closed-loop control.\n\n    Args:\n        sim: HVACSimulator instance (already initialized from calibration)\n        tuned_gains: Dictionary with Kp, Ki, Kd\n        setpoint: Target temperature\n        duration: Control duration (seconds)\n        start_time: Starting time offset (from calibration)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with control log\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"\\nRunning closed-loop control to {setpoint}C for {duration}s...\")\n\n    # Initialize PID controller\n    dt = sim.get_dt()\n    controller = PIDController(\n        Kp=tuned_gains[\"Kp\"],\n        Ki=tuned_gains[\"Ki\"],\n        Kd=tuned_gains[\"Kd\"],\n        dt=dt,\n        min_output=0.0,\n        max_output=100.0\n    )\n\n    # Collect control data\n    data = []\n    steps = int(duration / dt)\n\n    for i in range(steps):\n        # Get current temperature from last simulator state\n        # (simulator already at state from calibration)\n        if i == 0:\n            # Get current temperature\n            result = sim.step(0.0)  # Take one step to get current state\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            current_time = start_time + result[\"time\"]\n        else:\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            current_time = start_time + result[\"time\"]\n\n        # Compute control output\n        error = setpoint - current_temp\n        heater_power = controller.compute(setpoint, current_temp)\n\n        # Apply control and step simulator\n        result = sim.step(heater_power)\n\n        # Log data\n        data.append({\n            \"time\": round(current_time, 2),\n            \"temperature\": round(current_temp, 4),\n            \"setpoint\": setpoint,\n            \"heater_power\": round(heater_power, 4),\n            \"error\": round(error, 4)\n        })\n\n        # Progress update\n        if (i + 1) % 100 == 0:\n            print(f\"  t={current_time:.1f}s: T={current_temp:.2f}C, P={heater_power:.1f}%, e={error:.2f}C\")\n\n    print(f\"Closed-loop control complete: {len(data)} data points\")\n\n    control_log = {\n        \"phase\": \"control\",\n        \"setpoint\": setpoint,\n        \"data\": data\n    }\n\n    return control_log\n\n\ndef calculate_metrics(control_log):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate performance metrics from control log.\n\n    Args:\n        control_log: Control log dictionary\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with performance metrics\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nCalculating performance metrics...\")\n\n    data = control_log[\"data\"]\n    setpoint = control_log[\"setpoint\"]\n\n    times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data])\n    temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data])\n    errors = np.array([d[\"error\"] for d in data])\n\n    initial_temp = temps[0]\n    initial_time = times[0]\n\n    # 1. Rise time (time to reach 90% of setpoint from initial)\n    target_90 = initial_temp + 0.9 * (setpoint - initial_temp)\n    idx_90 = np.where(temps >= target_90)[0]\n    if len(idx_90) > 0:\n        rise_time = times[idx_90[0]] - initial_time\n    else:\n        rise_time = None\n\n    # 2. Overshoot (percentage above setpoint)\n    max_temp = np.max(temps)\n    if max_temp > setpoint:\n        overshoot = (max_temp - setpoint) / setpoint\n    else:\n        overshoot = 0.0\n\n    # 3. Settling time (time to stay within ±2% of setpoint)\n    tolerance = 0.02\n    tolerance_value = abs(setpoint * tolerance)\n    lower_bound = setpoint - tolerance_value\n    upper_bound = setpoint + tolerance_value\n\n    in_band = (temps >= lower_bound) & (temps <= upper_bound)\n\n    # Find last time it was outside the band\n    outside_indices = np.where(~in_band)[0]\n    if len(outside_indices) > 0:\n        settling_idx = outside_indices[-1] + 1\n        if settling_idx < len(times):\n            settling_time = times[settling_idx] - initial_time\n        else:\n            settling_time = None\n    else:\n        # Always in band\n        settling_time = 0.0\n\n    # 4. Calibration test to characterize room dynamics\n2. Parameter estimation from calibration data\n3. PID controller gain tuning\n4. Closed-loop control to reach setpoint\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\nfrom hvac_simulator import HVACSimulator\n\n\nclass PIDController:\n    \"\"\"PID controller with anti-windup.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, dt, min_output=0.0, max_output=100.0):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.dt = dt\n        self.min_output = min_output\n        self.max_output = max_output\n\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n    def compute(self, setpoint, measurement):\n        \"\"\"Compute PID output with anti-windup.\"\"\"\n        error = setpoint - measurement\n\n        # Proportional term\n        P = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term (will apply anti-windup below)\n        self.integral += error * self.dt\n        I = self.Ki * self.integral\n\n        # Derivative term\n        derivative = (error - self.prev_error) / self.dt\n        D = self.Kd * derivative\n\n        self.prev_error = error\n\n        # Compute unclamped output\n        output = P + I + D\n\n        # Apply output limits\n        clamped_output = np.clip(output, self.min_output, self.max_output)\n\n        # Anti-windup: back-calculate integral\n        if self.Ki != 0 and output != clamped_output:\n            self.integral -= (output - clamped_output) / self.Ki\n\n        return clamped_output\n\n    def reset(self):\n        \"\"\"Reset controller state.\"\"\"\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n\ndef run_calibration(sim, heater_power=50.0, duration=30.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run open-loop calibration test.\n\n    Args:\n        sim: HVACSimulator instance\n        heater_power: Constant heater power for test (%)\n        duration: Test duration (seconds)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with calibration log\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Running calibration test: {heater_power}% power for {duration}s\")\n\n    # Reset simulator\n    initial_temp = sim.reset()\n    print(f\"Initial temperature: {initial_temp:.2f}C\")\n\n    # Collect data with heater off first (initial condition)\n    data = []\n    data.append({\n        \"time\": 0.0,\n        \"temperature\": initial_temp,\n        \"heater_power\": 0.0\n    })\n\n    # Run open-loop test\n    results = sim.run_open_loop(heater_power, duration)\n\n    # Add results to data\n    for result in results:\n        data.append({\n            \"time\": result[\"time\"],\n            \"temperature\": result[\"temperature\"],\n            \"heater_power\": result[\"heater_power\"]\n        })\n\n    print(f\"Calibration complete: {len(data)} data points collected\")\n\n    calibration_log = {\n        \"phase\": \"calibration\",\n        \"heater_power_test\": heater_power,\n        \"data\": data\n    }\n\n    return calibration_log\n\n\ndef estimate_parameters(calibration_log):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate system parameters from calibration data.\n\n    Args:\n        calibration_log: Calibration log dictionary\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with estimated parameters\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"Estimating system parameters from calibration data...\")\n\n    data = calibration_log[\"data\"]\n    heater_power = calibration_log[\"heater_power_test\"]\n\n    # Extract time and temperature arrays (skip first point where heater is off)\n    times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data[1:]])\n    temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data[1:]])\n\n    # Adjust time to start from 0\n    times = times - times[0]\n\n    # Initial temperature (before step)\n    T0 = data[0][\"temperature\"]\n\n    # Define first-order step response model\n    def first_order_response(t, K, tau):\n        \"\"\"First-order step response: T(t) = T0 + K*u*(1 - exp(-t/tau))\"\"\"\n        return T0 + K * heater_power * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n    # Initial parameter guesses\n    K_guess = 0.1  # Process gain\n    tau_guess = 30.0  # Time constant\n\n    try:\n        # Fit the model\n        params, covariance = curve_fit(\n            first_order_response,\n            times,\n            temps,\n            p0=[K_guess, tau_guess],\n            bounds=([0.01, 5.0], [0.5, 100.0]),\n            maxfev=5000\n        )\n\n        K_est, tau_est = params\n\n        # Calculate fit quality\n        y_pred = first_order_response(times, K_est, tau_est)\n        ss_res = np.sum((temps - y_pred) ** 2)\n        ss_tot = np.sum((temps - np.mean(temps)) ** 2)\n        r_squared = 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n\n        # Fitting error (RMSE)\n        fitting_error = np.sqrt(np.mean((temps - y_pred) ** 2))\n\n        print(f\"Estimated K = {K_est:.4f}, tau = {tau_est:.2f}s\")\n        print(f\"R² = {r_squared:.4f}, RMSE = {fitting_error:.4f}\")\n\n        estimated_params = {\n            \"K\": round(K_est, 6),\n            \"tau\": round(tau_est, 4),\n            \"r_squared\": round(r_squared, 6),\n            \"fitting_error\": round(fitting_error, 6)\n        }\n\n        return estimated_params\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Parameter estimation failed: {e}\")\n        raise\n\n\ndef calculate_controller_gains(estimated_params, lambda_factor=1.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate PID controller gains using lambda tuning.\n\n    Args:\n        estimated_params: Dictionary with K and tau\n        lambda_factor: Tuning parameter (multiplier of tau)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with controller gains\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"Calculating controller gains...\")\n\n    K = estimated_params[\"K\"]\n    tau = estimated_params[\"tau\"]\n\n    # Lambda tuning (IMC-based)\n    lambda_val = lambda_factor * tau\n\n    Kp = tau / (K * lambda_val)\n    Ki = 1.0 / lambda_val\n    Kd = 0.0  # Not needed for thermal systems\n\n    print(f\"Controller gains: Kp={Kp:.4f}, Ki={Ki:.4f}, Kd={Kd:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Lambda = {lambda_val:.2f}s\")\n\n    tuned_gains = {\n        \"Kp\": round(Kp, 6),\n        \"Ki\": round(Ki, 6),\n        \"Kd\": round(Kd, 6),\n        \"lambda\": round(lambda_val, 4)\n    }\n\n    return tuned_gains\n\n\ndef run_closed_loop_control(sim, tuned_gains, setpoint, duration=150.0, start_time=0.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run closed-loop control.\n\n    Args:\n        sim: HVACSimulator instance (already initialized from calibration)\n        tuned_gains: Dictionary with Kp, Ki, Kd\n        setpoint: Target temperature\n        duration: Control duration (seconds)\n        start_time: Starting time offset (from calibration)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with control log\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"\\nRunning closed-loop control to {setpoint}C for {duration}s...\")\n\n    # Get current temperature\n            result = sim.step(0.0)  # Take one step to get current state\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            current_time = start_time + result[\"time\"]\n        else:\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            current_time = start_time + result[\"time\"]\n\n        # Compute control output\n        error = setpoint - current_temp\n        heater_power = controller.compute(setpoint, current_temp)\n\n        # Apply control and step simulator\n        result = sim.step(heater_power)\n\n        # Log data\n        data.append({\n            \"time\": round(current_time, 2),\n            \"temperature\": round(current_temp, 4),\n            \"setpoint\": setpoint,\n            \"heater_power\": round(heater_power, 4),\n            \"error\": round(error, 4)\n        })\n\n        # 1. Rise time (time to reach 90% of setpoint from initial)\n    target_90 = initial_temp + 0.9 * (setpoint - initial_temp)\n    idx_90 = np.where(temps >= target_90)[0]\n    if len(idx_90) > 0:\n        rise_time = times[idx_90[0]] - initial_time\n    else:\n        rise_time = None\n\n    # 2. Overshoot (percentage above setpoint)\n    max_temp = np.max(temps)\n    if max_temp > setpoint:\n        overshoot = (max_temp - setpoint) / setpoint\n    else:\n        overshoot = 0.0\n\n    # 3. Settling time (time to stay within ±2% of setpoint)\n    tolerance = 0.02\n    tolerance_value = abs(setpoint * tolerance)\n    lower_bound = setpoint - tolerance_value\n    upper_bound = setpoint + tolerance_value\n\n    in_band = (temps >= lower_bound) & (temps <= upper_bound)\n\n    # Find last time it was outside the band\n    outside_indices = np.where(~in_band)[0]\n    if len(outside_indices) > 0:\n        settling_idx = outside_indices[-1] + 1\n        if settling_idx < len(times):\n            settling_time = times[settling_idx] - initial_time\n        else:\n            settling_time = None\n    else:\n        # Always in band\n        settling_time = 0.0\n\n    # 4. Steady-state error (average of last 20% of data)\n    last_n = max(20, len(temps) // 5)\n    final_temps = temps[-last_n:]\n    avg_final = np.mean(final_temps)\n    steady_state_error = abs(setpoint - avg_final)\n\n    # 5. Closed-loop control to reach setpoint\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\nfrom hvac_simulator import HVACSimulator\n\n\nclass PIDController:\n    \"\"\"PID controller with anti-windup.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, dt, min_output=0.0, max_output=100.0):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.dt = dt\n        self.min_output = min_output\n        self.max_output = max_output\n\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n    def compute(self, setpoint, measurement):\n        \"\"\"Compute PID output with anti-windup.\"\"\"\n        error = setpoint - measurement\n\n        # Proportional term\n        P = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term (will apply anti-windup below)\n        self.integral += error * self.dt\n        I = self.Ki * self.integral\n\n        # Derivative term\n        derivative = (error - self.prev_error) / self.dt\n        D = self.Kd * derivative\n\n        self.prev_error = error\n\n        # Compute unclamped output\n        output = P + I + D\n\n        # Apply output limits\n        clamped_output = np.clip(output, self.min_output, self.max_output)\n\n        # Anti-windup: back-calculate integral\n        if self.Ki != 0 and output != clamped_output:\n            self.integral -= (output - clamped_output) / self.Ki\n\n        return clamped_output\n\n    def reset(self):\n        \"\"\"Reset controller state.\"\"\"\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n\ndef run_calibration(sim, heater_power=50.0, duration=30.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run open-loop calibration test.\n\n    Args:\n        sim: HVACSimulator instance\n        heater_power: Constant heater power for test (%)\n        duration: Test duration (seconds)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with calibration log\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Running calibration test: {heater_power}% power for {duration}s\")\n\n    # Reset simulator\n    initial_temp = sim.reset()\n    print(f\"Initial temperature: {initial_temp:.2f}C\")\n\n    # Collect data with heater off first (initial condition)\n    data = []\n    data.append({\n        \"time\": 0.0,\n        \"temperature\": initial_temp,\n        \"heater_power\": 0.0\n    })\n\n    # Run open-loop test\n    results = sim.run_open_loop(heater_power, duration)\n\n    # Add results to data\n    for result in results:\n        data.append({\n            \"time\": result[\"time\"],\n            \"temperature\": result[\"temperature\"],\n            \"heater_power\": result[\"heater_power\"]\n        })\n\n    print(f\"Calibration complete: {len(data)} data points collected\")\n\n    calibration_log = {\n        \"phase\": \"calibration\",\n        \"heater_power_test\": heater_power,\n        \"data\": data\n    }\n\n    return calibration_log\n\n\ndef estimate_parameters(calibration_log):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate system parameters from calibration data.\n\n    Args:\n        calibration_log: Calibration log dictionary\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with estimated parameters\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"Estimating system parameters from calibration data...\")\n\n    data = calibration_log[\"data\"]\n    heater_power = calibration_log[\"heater_power_test\"]\n\n    # Extract time and temperature arrays (skip first point where heater is off)\n    times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data[1:]])\n    temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data[1:]])\n\n    # Adjust time to start from 0\n    times = times - times[0]\n\n    # Initial temperature (before step)\n    T0 = data[0][\"temperature\"]\n\n    # Define first-order step response model\n    def first_order_response(t, K, tau):\n        \"\"\"First-order step response: T(t) = T0 + K*u*(1 - exp(-t/tau))\"\"\"\n        return T0 + K * heater_power * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n    # Initial parameter guesses\n    K_guess = 0.1  # Process gain\n    tau_guess = 30.0  # Time constant\n\n    try:\n        # Fit the model\n        params, covariance = curve_fit(\n            first_order_response,\n            times,\n            temps,\n            p0=[K_guess, tau_guess],\n            bounds=([0.01, 5.0], [0.5, 100.0]),\n            maxfev=5000\n        )\n\n        K_est, tau_est = params\n\n        # Calculate fit quality\n        y_pred = first_order_response(times, K_est, tau_est)\n        ss_res = np.sum((temps - y_pred) ** 2)\n        ss_tot = np.sum((temps - np.mean(temps)) ** 2)\n        r_squared = 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n\n        # Fitting error (RMSE)\n        fitting_error = np.sqrt(np.mean((temps - y_pred) ** 2))\n\n        print(f\"Estimated K = {K_est:.4f}, tau = {tau_est:.2f}s\")\n        print(f\"R² = {r_squared:.4f}, RMSE = {fitting_error:.4f}\")\n\n        estimated_params = {\n            \"K\": round(K_est, 6),\n            \"tau\": round(tau_est, 4),\n            \"r_squared\": round(r_squared, 6),\n            \"fitting_error\": round(fitting_error, 6)\n        }\n\n        return estimated_params\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Parameter estimation failed: {e}\")\n        raise\n\n\ndef calculate_controller_gains(estimated_params, lambda_factor=1.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate PID controller gains using lambda tuning.\n\n    Args:\n        estimated_params: Dictionary with K and tau\n        lambda_factor: Tuning parameter (multiplier of tau)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with controller gains\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"Calculating controller gains...\")\n\n    K = estimated_params[\"K\"]\n    tau = estimated_params[\"tau\"]\n\n    # Lambda tuning (IMC-based)\n    lambda_val = lambda_factor * tau\n\n    Kp = tau / (K * lambda_val)\n    Ki = 1.0 / lambda_val\n    Kd = 0.0  # Not needed for thermal systems\n\n    print(f\"Controller gains: Kp={Kp:.4f}, Ki={Ki:.4f}, Kd={Kd:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Lambda = {lambda_val:.2f}s\")\n\n    tuned_gains = {\n        \"Kp\": round(Kp, 6),\n        \"Ki\": round(Ki, 6),\n        \"Kd\": round(Kd, 6),\n        \"lambda\": round(lambda_val, 4)\n    }\n\n    return tuned_gains\n\n\ndef run_closed_loop_control(sim, tuned_gains, setpoint, duration=150.0, start_time=0.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run closed-loop control.\n\n    Args:\n        sim: HVACSimulator instance (already initialized from calibration)\n        tuned_gains: Dictionary with Kp, Ki, Kd\n        setpoint: Target temperature\n        duration: Control duration (seconds)\n        start_time: Starting time offset (from calibration)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with control log\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"\\nRunning closed-loop control to {setpoint}C for {duration}s...\")\n\n    # Initialize PID controller\n    dt = sim.get_dt()\n    controller = PIDController(\n        Kp=tuned_gains[\"Kp\"],\n        Ki=tuned_gains[\"Ki\"],\n        Kd=tuned_gains[\"Kd\"],\n        dt=dt,\n        min_output=0.0,\n        max_output=100.0\n    )\n\n    # Collect control data\n    data = []\n    steps = int(duration / dt)\n\n    for i in range(steps):\n        # Get current temperature from last simulator state\n        # (simulator already at state from calibration)\n        if i == 0:\n            # Get current temperature\n            result = sim.step(0.0)  # Take one step to get current state\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            current_time = start_time + result[\"time\"]\n        else:\n            current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n            current_time = start_time + result[\"time\"]\n\n        # Compute control output\n        error = setpoint - current_temp\n        heater_power = controller.compute(setpoint, current_temp)\n\n        # Apply control and step simulator\n        result = sim.step(heater_power)\n\n        # Log data\n        data.append({\n            \"time\": round(current_time, 2),\n            \"temperature\": round(current_temp, 4),\n            \"setpoint\": setpoint,\n            \"heater_power\": round(heater_power, 4),\n            \"error\": round(error, 4)\n        })\n\n        # Progress update\n        if (i + 1) % 100 == 0:\n            print(f\"  t={current_time:.1f}s: T={current_temp:.2f}C, P={heater_power:.1f}%, e={error:.2f}C\")\n\n    print(f\"Closed-loop control complete: {len(data)} data points\")\n\n    control_log = {\n        \"phase\": \"control\",\n        \"setpoint\": setpoint,\n        \"data\": data\n    }\n\n    return control_log\n\n\ndef calculate_metrics(control_log):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate performance metrics from control log.\n\n    Args:\n        control_log: Control log dictionary\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with performance metrics\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nCalculating performance metrics...\")\n\n    data = control_log[\"data\"]\n    setpoint = control_log[\"setpoint\"]\n\n    times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data])\n    temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data])\n    errors = np.array([d[\"error\"] for d in data])\n\n    initial_temp = temps[0]\n    initial_time = times[0]\n\n    # 1. Rise time (time to reach 90% of setpoint from initial)\n    target_90 = initial_temp + 0.9 * (setpoint - initial_temp)\n    idx_90 = np.where(temps >= target_90)[0]\n    if len(idx_90) > 0:\n        rise_time = times[idx_90[0]] - initial_time\n    else:\n        rise_time = None\n\n    # 2. Overshoot (percentage above setpoint)\n    max_temp = np.max(temps)\n    if max_temp > setpoint:\n        overshoot = (max_temp - setpoint) / setpoint\n    else:\n        overshoot = 0.0\n\n    # 3. Settling time (time to stay within ±2% of setpoint)\n    tolerance = 0.02\n    tolerance_value = abs(setpoint * tolerance)\n    lower_bound = setpoint - tolerance_value\n    upper_bound = setpoint + tolerance_value\n\n    in_band = (temps >= lower_bound) & (temps <= upper_bound)\n\n    # Find last time it was outside the band\n    outside_indices = np.where(~in_band)[0]\n    if len(outside_indices) > 0:\n        settling_idx = outside_indices[-1] + 1\n        if settling_idx < len(times):\n            settling_time = times[settling_idx] - initial_time\n        else:\n            settling_time = None\n    else:\n        # Always in band\n        settling_time = 0.0\n\n    # 4. Steady-state error (average of last 20% of data)\n    last_n = max(20, len(temps) // 5)\n    final_temps = temps[-last_n:]\n    avg_final = np.mean(final_temps)\n    steady_state_error = abs(setpoint - avg_final)\n\n    # 5. Maximum temperature\n    max_temp_reached = np.max(temps)\n\n    print(f\"  Rise time: {rise_time:.2f}s\" if rise_time else \"  Rise time: N/A\")\n    print(f\"  Overshoot: {overshoot*100:.2f}%\")\n    print(f\"  Settling time: {settling_time:.2f}s\" if settling_time else \"  Settling time: N/A\")\n    print(f\"  Steady-state error: {steady_state_error:.4f}C\")\n    print(f\"  Max temperature: {max_temp_reached:.2f}C\")\n\n    metrics = {\n        \"rise_time\": round(rise_time, 4) if rise_time is not None else None,\n        \"overshoot\": round(overshoot, 6),\n        \"settling_time\": round(settling_time, 4) if settling_time is not None else None,\n        \"steady_state_error\": round(steady_state_error, 6),\n        \"max_temp\": round(max_temp_reached, 4)\n    }\n\n    return Calibration test to characterize room dynamics\n2. Parameter estimation from calibration data\n3. PID controller gain tuning\n4. Closed-loop control to reach setpoint\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\nfrom hvac_simulator import HVACSimulator\n\n\nclass PIDController:\n    \"\"\"PID controller with anti-windup.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, dt, min_output=0.0, max_output=100.0):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.dt = dt\n        self.min_output = min_output\n        self.max_output = max_output\n\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n    def compute(self, setpoint, measurement):\n        \"\"\"Compute PID output with anti-windup.\"\"\"\n        error = setpoint - measurement\n\n        # Proportional term\n        P = self.Kp * error\n\n        # Integral term (will apply anti-windup below)\n        self.integral += error * self.dt\n        I = self.Ki * self.integral\n\n        # Derivative term\n        derivative = (error - self.prev_error) / self.dt\n        D = self.Kd * derivative\n\n        self.prev_error = error\n\n        # Compute unclamped output\n        output = P + I + D\n\n        # Apply output limits\n        clamped_output = np.clip(output, self.min_output, self.max_output)\n\n        # Anti-windup: back-calculate integral\n        if self.Ki != 0 and output != clamped_output:\n            self.integral -= (output - clamped_output) / self.Ki\n\n        return clamped_output\n\n    def reset(self):\n        \"\"\"Reset controller state.\"\"\"\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n\ndef run_calibration(sim, heater_power=50.0, duration=30.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run open-loop calibration test.\n\n    Args:\n        sim: HVACSimulator instance\n        heater_power: Constant heater power for test (%)\n        duration: Test duration (seconds)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with calibration log\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Running calibration test: {heater_power}% power for {duration}s\")\n\n    # Reset simulator\n    initial_temp = sim.reset()\n    print(f\"Initial temperature: {initial_temp:.2f}C\")\n\n    # Collect data with heater off first (initial condition)\n    data = []\n    data.append({\n        \"time\": 0.0,\n        \"temperature\": initial_temp,\n        \"heater_power\": 0.0\n    })\n\n    # Run open-loop test\n    results = sim.run_open_loop(heater_power, duration)\n\n    # Add results to data\n    for result in results:\n        data.append({\n            \"time\": result[\"time\"],\n            \"temperature\": result[\"temperature\"],\n            \"heater_power\": result[\"heater_power\"]\n        })\n\n    print(f\"Calibration complete: {len(data)} data points collected\")\n\n    calibration_log = {\n        \"phase\": \"calibration\",\n        \"heater_power_test\": heater_power,\n        \"data\": data\n    }\n\n    return calibration_log\n\n\ndef estimate_parameters(calibration_log):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate system parameters from calibration data.\n\n    Args:\n        calibration_log: Calibration log dictionary\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with estimated parameters\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"Estimating system parameters from calibration data...\")\n\n    data = calibration_log[\"data\"]\n    heater_power = calibration_log[\"heater_power_test\"]\n\n    # Extract time and temperature arrays (skip first point where heater is off)\n    times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data[1:]])\n    temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data[1:]])\n\n    # Adjust time to start from 0\n    times = times - times[0]\n\n    # Initial temperature (before step)\n    T0 = data[0][\"temperature\"]\n\n    # Define first-order step response model\n    def first_order_response(t, K, tau):\n        \"\"\"First-order step response: T(t) = T0 + K*u*(1 - exp(-t/tau))\"\"\"\n        return T0 + K * heater_power * (1 - np.exp(-t / tau))\n\n    # Initial parameter guesses\n    K_guess = 0.1  # Process gain\n    tau_guess = 30.0  # Time constant\n\n    try:\n        # Fit the model\n        params, covariance = curve_fit(\n            first_order_response,\n            times,\n            temps,\n            p0=[K_guess, tau_guess],\n            bounds=([0.01, 5.0], [0.5, 100.0]),\n            maxfev=5000\n        )\n\n        K_est, tau_est = params\n\n        # Calculate fit quality\n        y_pred = first_order_response(times, K_est, tau_est)\n        ss_res = np.sum((temps - y_pred) ** 2)\n        ss_tot = np.sum((temps - np.mean(temps)) ** 2)\n        r_squared = 1 - (ss_res / ss_tot)\n\n        # Fitting error (RMSE)\n        fitting_error = np.sqrt(np.mean((temps - y_pred) ** 2))\n\n        print(f\"Estimated K = {K_est:.4f}, tau = {tau_est:.2f}s\")\n        print(f\"R² = {r_squared:.4f}, RMSE = {fitting_error:.4f}\")\n\n        estimated_params = {\n            \"K\": round(K_est, 6),\n            \"tau\": round(tau_est, 4),\n            \"r_squared\": round(r_squared, 6),\n            \"fitting_error\": round(fitting_error, 6)\n        }\n\n        return estimated_params\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        print(f\"Parameter estimation failed: {e}\")\n        raise\n\n\ndef calculate_controller_gains(estimated_params, lambda_factor=1.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate PID controller gains using lambda tuning.\n\n    Args:\n        estimated_params: Dictionary with K and tau\n        lambda_factor: Tuning parameter (multiplier of tau)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with controller gains\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"Calculating controller gains...\")\n\n    K = estimated_params[\"K\"]\n    tau = estimated_params[\"tau\"]\n\n    # Lambda tuning (IMC-based)\n    lambda_val = lambda_factor * tau\n\n    Kp = tau / (K * lambda_val)\n    Ki = 1.0 / lambda_val\n    Kd = 0.0  # Not needed for thermal systems\n\n    print(f\"Controller gains: Kp={Kp:.4f}, Ki={Ki:.4f}, Kd={Kd:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"Lambda = {lambda_val:.2f}s\")\n\n    tuned_gains = {\n        \"Kp\": round(Kp, 6),\n        \"Ki\": round(Ki, 6),\n        \"Kd\": round(Kd, 6),\n        \"lambda\": round(lambda_val, 4)\n    }\n\n    return tuned_gains\n\n\ndef run_closed_loop_control(sim, tuned_gains, setpoint, duration=150.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run closed-loop control.\n\n    Args:\n        sim: HVACSimulator instance\n        tuned_gains: Dictionary with Kp, Ki, Kd\n        setpoint: Target temperature\n        duration: Control duration (seconds)\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with control log\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Running closed-loop control to {setpoint}C for {duration}s...\")\n\n    # Collect control data\n    data = []\n    steps = int(duration / dt)\n\n    # Get initial measurement\n    current_temp = initial_temp\n\n    for i in range(steps):\n        # Compute control output\n        error = setpoint - current_temp\n        heater_power = controller.compute(setpoint, current_temp)\n\n        # Apply control and step simulator\n        result = sim.step(heater_power)\n        current_temp = result[\"temperature\"]\n        current_time = result[\"time\"]\n\n        # Log data\n        data.append({\n            \"time\": round(current_time, 2),\n            \"temperature\": round(current_temp, 4),\n            \"setpoint\": setpoint,\n            \"heater_power\": round(heater_power, 4),\n            \"error\": round(error, 4)\n        })\n\n        # Progress update\n        if (i + 1) % 100 == 0:\n            print(f\"  t={current_time:.1f}s: T={current_temp:.2f}C, P={heater_power:.1f}%, e={error:.2f}C\")\n\n    print(f\"Closed-loop control complete: {len(data)} data points\")\n\n    control_log = {\n        \"phase\": \"control\",\n        \"setpoint\": setpoint,\n        \"data\": data\n    }\n\n    return control_log\n\n\ndef calculate_metrics(control_log):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate performance metrics from control log.\n\n    Args:\n        control_log: Control log dictionary\n\n    Returns:\n        Dictionary with performance metrics\n    \"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nCalculating performance metrics...\")\n\n    data = control_log[\"data\"]\n    setpoint = control_log[\"setpoint\"]\n\n    times = np.array([d[\"time\"] for d in data])\n    temps = np.array([d[\"temperature\"] for d in data])\n    errors = np.array([d[\"error\"] for d in data])\n\n    initial_temp = temps[0]\n    initial_time = times[0]\n\n    # 1. Rise time (time to reach 90% of setpoint from initial)\n    target_90 = initial_temp + 0.9 * (setpoint - initial_temp)\n    idx_90 = np.where(temps >= target_90)[0]\n    if len(idx_90) > 0:\n        rise_time = times[idx_90[0]] - initial_time\n    else:\n        rise_time = None\n\n    # 2. Overshoot (percentage above setpoint)\n    max_temp = np.max(temps)\n    if max_temp > setpoint:\n        overshoot = (max_temp - setpoint) / setpoint\n    else:\n        overshoot = 0.0\n\n    # 3. Settling time (time to stay within ±2% of setpoint)\n    tolerance = 0.02\n    tolerance_value = abs(setpoint * tolerance)\n    lower_bound = setpoint - tolerance_value\n    upper_bound = setpoint + tolerance_value\n\n    in_band = (temps >= lower_bound) & (temps <= upper_bound)\n\n    # Find last time it was outside the band\n    outside_indices = np.where(~in_band)[0]\n    if len(outside_indices) > 0:\n        settling_idx = outside_indices[-1] + 1\n        if settling_idx < len(times):\n            settling_time = times[settling_idx] - initial_time\n        else:\n            settling_time = None\n    else:\n        # Always in band\n        settling_time = 0.0\n\n    # 4. Steady-state error (average of last 20% of data)\n    last_n = max(20, len(temps) // 5)\n    final_temps = temps[-last_n:]\n    avg_final = np.mean(final_temps)\n    steady_state_error = abs(setpoint - avg_final)\n\n    # 5. Maximum temperature\n    max_temp_reached = np.max(temps)\n\n    print(f\"  Rise time: {rise_time:.2f}s\" if rise_time else \"  Rise time: N/A\")\n    print(f\"  Overshoot: {overshoot*100:.2f}%\")\n    print(f\"  Settling time: {settling_time:.2f}s\" if settling_time else \"  Settling time: N/A\")\n    print(f\"  Steady-state error: {steady_state_error:.4f}C\")\n    print(f\"  Max temperature: {max_temp_reached:.2f}C\")\n\n    metrics = {\n        \"rise_time\": round(rise_time, 4) if rise_time is not None else None,\n        \"overshoot\": round(overshoot, 6),\n        \"settling_time\": round(settling_time, 4) if settling_time is not None else None,\n        \"steady_state_error\": round(steady_state_error, 6),\n        \"max_temp\": round(max_temp_reached, 4)\n    }\n\n    return metrics\n\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Main execution function.\"\"\"\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"Temperature Controller Implementation\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    # Closed-loop control to reach setpoint\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\nfrom hvac_simulator import HVACSimulator\n\n\nclass PIDController:\n    \"\"\"PID controller with anti-windup.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, Kp, Ki, Kd, dt, min_output=0.0, max_output=100.0):\n        self.Kp = Kp\n        self.Ki = Ki\n        self.Kd = Kd\n        self.dt = dt\n        self.min_output = min_output\n        self.max_output = max_output\n\n        self.integral = 0.0\n        self.prev_error = 0.0\n\n    def compute(self, setpoint, measurement):\n        \"\"\"Compute PID output with anti-windup.\"\"\"\n        error = setpoint - measurement\n\n        #
